{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}(\overline{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}})=\inf\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}):\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{F}$}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}\right\rVert_{2}=\min_{x}\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{F}$}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}\right\rVert\}
. From the (3) of Assumption
1
, it follows that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}(\overline{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}})\geq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*}}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*}}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*}}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*}}$}}})
. Similarly, there exists
\overline{\lambda}
such that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{\lambda}}^{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}})\geq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
.
∎

In practice, only the bounded minimizers are useful. Next, the minimizers in a bounded region
\Omega=[0,M]^{D_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}}\subset\mathbb{R}^{D_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}}
are studied. Since
\textstyle{R}
is coercive, there exists
M
such that
\overline{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}}
and
\underline{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}}
in Theorem
3.4
can be found in
\Omega
.

Theorem 3.6
.

For fixed
\lambda>0
, for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\delta>0
such that when
|\overline{\lambda}-\lambda|<\delta
, for any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}\in\argmin_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}})
, there exists
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{\lambda}}\in\argmin_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}})
that satisfy
\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{\lambda}}\right\rVert_{2}<\varepsilon
.

Proof.

First, due to the uniform continuity of
\textstyle{F}
and
\textstyle{R}
on the closed region
\Omega
, there exists constants
C_{1},C_{2},C_{3}>0
that do not depend on
\textstyle{x}
such that

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &\left|\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{F}$}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{1}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}/2-\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{F}$}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{2}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}/2\right|<C_{1}\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{1}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{2}\right\rVert_{2}\\
&\left|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{1})-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{2})\right|<C_{2}\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{1}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{2}\right\rVert_{2},\\
&{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}})<C_{3}.\end{aligned}

Then for any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{1},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{2}\in\Omega
and
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
,
\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}})
satisfy

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{1};\lambda)-\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{2};\lambda)\right|\leq(C_{1}+C_{2}\lambda)\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{1}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{2}\right\rVert_{2}

(25)

\displaystyle\left|\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda_{1})-\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda_{2})\right|\leq C_{3}|\lambda_{1}-\lambda_{2}|.

For any
\lambda>0
, there are three cases of the number of the minimizers of
\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}})
in
\Omega
: unique, finite, or infinite. These cases are discussed respectively in the following.

(1)

When the minimizer
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}=\argmin_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}})
is unique, for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\delta_{0}>0
such that for all
\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}\right\rVert_{2}\geq\varepsilon
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)\geq\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda};\lambda)+\delta_{0}.

Let
\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}},\varepsilon)
denotes the region
\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{0}\in\Omega:\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{0}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\right\rVert<\varepsilon\}
. Actually, due to that
\Omega/\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda},\varepsilon)
is closed,
\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega/\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda},\varepsilon)}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)>\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)=\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda};\lambda)
. Then any
\delta_{0}
that satisfies
0<\delta_{0}\leq\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega/\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda},\varepsilon)}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)-\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda};\lambda)
fulfill the requirements.

Let
\delta=\frac{\delta_{0}}{2C_{3}}
. Then for any
\overline{\lambda}
that satisfies
\lambda-\delta<\overline{\lambda}<\lambda
, for any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega/\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda},\varepsilon)
, it is obtained that

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)-C_{3}(\lambda-\overline{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda};\lambda)+\delta_{0}-\delta_{0}/2

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda};\overline{\lambda})+\delta_{0}/2,

where the monotonicity of minimization
\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{;}\lambda)
for fixed
\textstyle{x}
with respect to
\lambda
is used.

If
\lambda<\overline{\lambda}<\lambda+\delta
, following the same way, for all
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega/\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda},\varepsilon)
, it is obtained

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda})

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda};\lambda)+\delta_{0}

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda};\overline{\lambda})-C_{3}(\overline{\lambda}-\lambda)+\delta_{0},

\displaystyle\geq\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda};\overline{\lambda})+\delta_{0}/2.

Anyway, when
|\overline{\lambda}-\lambda|<\delta
, for any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega/\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda};\varepsilon)
,
\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda})\geq\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda};\overline{\lambda})+\delta_{0}/2
. Therefore,

\displaystyle\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda})\leq\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda},\varepsilon)}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda})\leq\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda};\overline{\lambda})<\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega/\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda},\varepsilon)}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda}),

which implies that the minimizer of
\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda})
is in
\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda},\varepsilon)
, that is,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{\lambda}}\in\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda},\varepsilon)
.

(2)

If there are finite minimizers of
\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)
that are denoted by
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}^{i}\ (i=1,2,\dots,N)
, and the minimization is denoted by
\tilde{L}
, for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exist
\delta_{0}
that satisfies

\displaystyle 0<\delta_{0}<\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega/(\cup_{i}\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}^{i},\varepsilon))}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)-\tilde{L}

such that for any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega/(\cup_{i}\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}^{i},\varepsilon))
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)\geq\tilde{L}+\delta_{0}.

Similarly, it can be proved that there exists
\delta
such that for any
|\lambda-\overline{\lambda}|<\delta
, the minimizers of
\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda})
satisfy
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{\lambda}}\in\cup_{i}\mathcal{B}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}^{i},\varepsilon)
.

(3)

If there exist infinite minimizers of
\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)
, let the set of these minimizers is denoted by
A
and the minimization is denoted by
\tilde{L}:=\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)\ ({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in A)
. Then due to the continuity of
\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)
with respect to
\textstyle{x}
,
A
is closed. Define set
\mathcal{B}(A,\varepsilon):=\{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega:\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{0}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\right\rVert_{2}<\varepsilon,\text{ for any }{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{0}\in A\}
. Then, since
\Omega/\mathcal{B}(A,\varepsilon)
is closed,
\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega/\mathcal{B}(A,\varepsilon)}-\tilde{L}>0
holds. And then for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists a positive
\delta_{0}
that satisfies that

\displaystyle\delta_{0}<\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega/\mathcal{B}(A,\varepsilon)}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)-\tilde{L}

such that for all
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega/\mathcal{B}(A,\varepsilon)
,
\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)\geq\tilde{L}+\delta_{0}
. Similarly, it can be proved that there exists positive
\delta\leq\frac{\delta_{0}}{2C_{3}}
such that for any
\overline{\lambda}
, if
|\overline{\lambda}-\lambda|<\delta
,

\displaystyle\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda})\geq\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{A};\overline{\lambda})+\delta_{0}/2

holds for any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega/\mathcal{B}(A,\varepsilon)
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{A}\in A
. It follows that

\displaystyle\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}},\overline{\lambda})\leq\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\mathcal{B}(A,\varepsilon)}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda})\leq\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{A}\in\mathcal{B}(A,\varepsilon)}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{A};\overline{\lambda})<\min_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega/\mathcal{B}(A,\varepsilon)}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda}).

Therefore, the minimizers of
\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda})
lie in
\mathcal{B}(A,\varepsilon)
, that is, for any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{A}\in A
, there exists a
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{\lambda}}\in\argmin_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{\lambda})
such that
\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{A}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{\lambda}}\right\rVert_{2}<\varepsilon
.

∎

Theorem 3.7
.

For any target image
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*}\in\Omega
and noisy observation
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{F}$}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}
, let Assumption

1
hold, then there exists a minimizer
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}^{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}
of
\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}})
in
\Omega
with
\lambda>0
such that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}^{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}})={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
.

Proof.

According to Corollary
3.5
, there exist
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
such that any
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{1}\in\argmin_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda_{1},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}})
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{2}\in\argmin_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda_{2},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}})
satisfy

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{1})\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*}),\,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{2})\geq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*}).

Define followings

\displaystyle\begin{aligned} &l=\sup\{\lambda:{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda})>{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})\text{ for all }{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}\in\argmin_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}})\},\\
&u=\inf\{\lambda:{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda})<{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})\text{ for all }{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}\in\argmin_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}})\}.\\
\end{aligned}

We can obtain
l\leq u
. Two cases are discussed separately.

(1)

l<u
.

Then for any
\lambda\in(l,u)
, according to Theorem
3.4
,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda})\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda})\geq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
hold for all minimizers
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}
of
\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda)
. Therefore,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda})={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
holds for any
\lambda\in(l,u)
.

(2)

l=u
.

From the uniformly continuous of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}})
in
\Omega
, for any
\varepsilon>0
, there exists
\delta
such that when
\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{1}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{2}\right\rVert_{2}<\delta
,
|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{1})-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{2})|<\varepsilon
holds. If
l=u
, according to Theorem
3.6
, there exist
\delta_{0}
, such that for any
\overline{l}<l=u<\overline{u}
that satisfies
|\overline{u}-\overline{l}|<\delta_{0}
, there exist
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{l}}\in\argmin_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{l},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}})
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{u}}\in\argmin_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\overline{u},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}})
that satisfy

\displaystyle\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{l}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{u}}\right\rVert_{2}<\delta.

And then
|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{l}})-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{u}})|<\varepsilon
. Moreover, due to the monotonicity of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda})
with respect to
\lambda
, any minimizer
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{l}\in\argmin_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\Omega}\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};l,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}})
satisfies that

\displaystyle{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{u}})\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{l})\leq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{l}}).

And combining
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{u}})<{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})<{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{l}})
, we can obtain that

\displaystyle|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{l})-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})|<|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{u}})-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\overline{l}})|<\varepsilon.

Therefore,
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{l})={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
.

∎

In reality, the true
\pi_{\theta_{\star}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
is unknown, so we can use training result
\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}|}\sum_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\mathcal{D}}\pi_{\theta_{\star}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}})
as an approximation of
\pi_{\theta_{\star}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
. According to the proof of Theorem
3.7
, the closed interval method can be used to find a
\lambda
that makes the regularization term
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda})
approximate
\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}|}\sum_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\mathcal{D}}-\log\pi_{\theta_{\star}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
.

4
Numerical implementation

In this section, to implement the rule (
17
), we present an incremental gradient descent algorithm with adaptive regularization parameters to address the estimation of
\textstyle{x}
from the minimization of (
14
). Moreover, a patch-based method is applied to address the reconstruction of larger-size images than training data, see
4.2
.

4.1
Incremental gradient descent algorithm

As we discussed previously, we use

C_{\mathcal{D},\theta^{\star}}:=\frac{1}{|\mathcal{D}|}\sum_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}\in\mathcal{D}}-\log\pi_{\theta_{\star}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})

(26)

in the rule (
17
) instead of unknown
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
in practice.
For fixed
\lambda
, the optimization problem (
16
) can be solved by the incremental gradient descent method, which alternately updates the data fidelity and regularization terms at each iteration
[
11
]
. In addition, combining the closed interval method to update
\lambda
, an algorithm to minimize (
16
) with adaptive regularization parameter is presented in
1
.

Algorithm 1

Incremental gradient descent with adaptive regularization

1:

Given fixed
Glow
model with the parameters
\theta_{\star}
and the prior
C_{\mathcal{D},\theta^{\star}}
, initial lower and upper bound of
\lambda
:
l_{0}
and
u_{0}
,
\lambda_{0}=l
, initial guess
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{0}
, tolerance
\varepsilon_{1}
and
\varepsilon_{2}
, rate
\beta\in(0,1)
.

2:

for

i=0,1,\dots

do

3:

{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{z}$}}}_{0}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{i}

4:

for

j=1,2,\dots

do

5:

\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{z}$}}}}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{z}$}}}_{j-1}-s\nabla(\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{F}$}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{z}$}}}_{j-1}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}\right\rVert_{2}^{2})
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{z}$}}}_{j}=\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{z}$}}}}-t\lambda_{i}\nabla{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}(\tilde{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{z}$}}}})

6:

end

for

7:

{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{i+1}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{z}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{z}$}}}_{j}

8:

Stop if one of these conditions are met: (i)
|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{i+1})-C|\leq\varepsilon_{1}
; (ii)
|u_{i}-l_{i}|\leq\varepsilon_{2}
;Otherwise, execute one of the following steps,

(i)

if
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{i+1})<C
, let
l_{i+1}=l_{i}
,
u_{i+1}=\lambda_{i}
, and
\lambda_{i+1}=\lambda_{i}-\beta|u_{i+1}-l_{i+1}|
;

(ii)

if
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{i+1})>C
, let
l_{i+1}=\lambda_{i}
,
u_{i+1}=u_{i}
, and
\lambda_{i+1}=\lambda_{i}+\beta|u_{i+1}-l_{i+1}|
.

9:

end

for

Remark
.

In algorithm
1
, it is assumed that
l_{0}
and
u_{0}
are lower and upper bound of
\lambda
, which means that the reconstruction
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{l_{0}}^{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}
and
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{u_{0}}^{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}
satisfies

{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{l_{0}})\geq C_{\mathcal{D},\theta^{\star}}\geq{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{u_{0}}).

(27)

Then from the initial lower and upper bound of
\lambda
, the closed interval between them keeps decreasing, and the interval size satisfies
|u_{i+1}-l_{i+1}|\leq(1-\beta)|u_{i}-l_{i}|
or
|u_{i+1}-l_{i+1}|\leq\beta|u_{i}-l_{i}|
. Therefore, after enough iterations,
|u_{i}-l_{i}|
or
|{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}(x^{i})-C_{\mathcal{D},\theta^{\star}}|
will meet the tolerance.

The lower and upper bound of
\lambda
can be precomputed. Updating
\lambda
using
\lambda=\beta\lambda\ (\beta>1)
and comparing the corresponding
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda})
and
C_{\mathcal{D},\theta^{\star}}
can find
\lambda
such that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda})\leq C_{\mathcal{D},\theta^{\star}}
. On the contrast, using
\lambda=\lambda\beta\ (\beta<1)
can find
\lambda
such that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda})\geq C_{\mathcal{D},\theta^{\star}}
.

The function called Autograd in deep learning packages, such as Pytorch helps compute the gradient of the regularization term.

As it is analyzed in Section
2.1
, to reconstruct the target image
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*}
, if the Gaussian noise level
\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}\right\rVert_{2}
is known, the regularization parameter should be
\lambda=\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}\right\rVert_{2}
. However, when the noise level is unknown, a parameter
\lambda
such that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}}_{\lambda})
matches the exact
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
is an admissible choice. Normally,
C_{\mathcal{D},\theta^{\star}}
that is from training data is used as an approximation of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
. Therefore, if the exact
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
is far away from the former, we can relax the expected range of
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}})
such that
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{\lambda}^{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{\eta}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{\eta}$}}}})\in({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})-a,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})+a)
for some relatively large
a>0
, in which only approximated
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{R}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{R}$}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*})
is needed, so the tolerance in Algorithm
1
don’t need to be small.

4.2
Patch-based regularization

The cost of training deep learning methods increases significantly with larger image sizes. This is particularly true for medical images, which are often three-dimensional. Therefore, we apply a patched-based regularization method
[
27
]
that only considers randomly selected patches of the same scale as the one used during the training phase. If the size of training images is
N_{x}\times N_{y}\times N_{z}
, to estimate the image of size
M_{x}\times M_{y}\times M_{z}
, the minimization objective is written as

\mathcal{L}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}};\lambda,{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{n}):=\frac{1}{2}\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{F}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{F}$}}}{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{n}\right\rVert_{2}^{2}-\frac{\lambda}{M}\sum_{m=1}^{M}\log(\pi_{\theta_{\star}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}[i_{m},j_{m},k_{m};N_{x},N_{y},N_{z}]))

(28)

where
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}[i_{m},j_{m},k_{m};N_{x},N_{y},N_{z}]
denotes the randomly selected three-dimensional submatrix of
\textstyle{x}
of scale
N_{x}\times N_{y}\times N_{z}
, that is,

{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}[i_{m},j_{m},k_{m};N_{x},N_{y},N_{z}]={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}(i_{m}:i_{m}+N_{x}-1,j_{m}:j_{m}+N_{y}-1,k_{m}:k_{m}+N_{z}-1).

(29)

For each iteration,
M
patches are randomly selected with the starting point of index
(i_{m},j_{m},k_{m})
in the discrete grids.

5
Simulations

Dataset and training

In this section, a lung dataset “ELCAP Public Lung Image Database” which consists of 50 whole-lung
Computer Tomography
scans
[
1
]
is used to verify the effectiveness and efficiency of the
Normalizing Flow Regularization
. Training data consists of 2400 images with size
32\times 32\times 32
selected from the last 40 patients. Validation data are selected from the first 10 patients. Before implementing training and reconstruction, Frangi vesselness filter is applied to obtain vessel-like structures and reduce background noise
[
16
]
. To increase the robustness of the network when only a few samples are available, data augmentation is an essential tool to increase the diversity of the dataset by applying random transformations, such as rotation, shift, and elastic deformations. For our three-dimensional images, a Python library TorchIO
[
34
]
is applied, in which random affine transform with scale
0.81.2
, rotation with degree
090
, flip and shift are considered. A three-layer
Glow
model is trained where each layer consists of
7
steps of flow with maximum hidden channels of
512
.

Measurement system

After training the
Glow
model, we apply the image prior to the reconstruction. Gaussian noise
\textstyle{n}
with level
\sigma
to the clear data is added as follows,

{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}_{n}={\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}}+{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{n}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{n}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{n}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{n}$}}},{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{n}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{n}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{n}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{n}$}}}\sim\mathcal{N}(\mathbf{0},(\sigma*\max{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{y}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{y}$}}})^{2}).

(30)

Following clinical specifications of limited-view measurement, transducer arrays in our simulations are positioned on a hemisphere as shown in Figure
1
. To investigate the efficacy of NFR in sparse measurements, we have implemented diverse sparse levels to produce measurements. These levels involve the deployment of transducer arrays uniformly distributed across distinct azimuth and polar angles in a hemisphere. To achieve this, we have implemented varying levels of sparsity, that is to use specific combinations of azimuth and polar angles
(256,16)
,
(128,12)
, and
(64,8)
, respectively. To evaluate the robustness to noise level, 0.01 and 0.05 Gaussian noise have been incorporated into the measurements, as detailed in Equation (
30
).

Figure 1:
Measurement system schematic. The reconstructed object (marked as the orange cube) is placed in the center of the coordinate system. The transducers (marked as red blocks) are placed on a hemispherical surface and arranged according to the azimuth (
0^{\circ}-360^{\circ}
) and polar (
0^{\circ}-90^{\circ}
) angles.

Evaluation

Considering our focus on the contrast of the image, as detailed in
[
24
,
48
]
, for the reconstruction
\overline{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}}
, the scaled and unbiased reconstruction is derived from

({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{r},a,b)=\argmin_{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}},a,b}\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}-a*\overline{{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}}-b\right\rVert_{2},

where
a,b\in\mathbb{R}
.
To assess the quality of image reconstruction, metrics including Structural Similarity Index (
Structural Similarity Index
)
[
44
]
, Peak Signal-to-Noise Ratio (
Peak Signal-to-Noise Ratio
), and relative reconstruction accuracy (
relative reconstruction accuracy
) of the scaled reconstructions are computed, in which
relative reconstruction accuracy
is defined as

\text{{\it{relative reconstruction accuracy}}}({\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{r})=\frac{\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}_{r}-{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*}\right\rVert_{2}}{\left\lVert{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*}\right\rVert_{2}}.

(31)

for ground truth
{\mathchoice{\mbox{\boldmath$\displaystyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\textstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptstyle{x}$}}{\mbox{\boldmath$\scriptscriptstyle{x}$}}}^{*}
.

In Section
5.1
, the effect of the regularization parameter on
relative reconstruction accuracy
and the regularization term is explored with different sparsity levels and noise levels, in which the image to be reconstructed is segmented from the first 10 patients and has the same size with training data. In Section
5.2
,
Normalizing Flow Regularization
method is applied to a larger image of size
128\times 128\times 128
that is segmented from the first 10 patients. Furthermore, a comparative analysis is performed between
Normalizing Flow Regularization
and two state-of-the-art image reconstruction approaches, post-processing
U-Net
and
Total Variation
regularization, in the case of different sparsity levels and noise levels.

5.1
Effect of regularization parameter

In this section,
Normalizing Flow Regularization
method is applied to an image selected from the validation data of the same size with training data,
32\times 32\times 32
, to study the effect of the regularization parameter on the reconstruction results. Various sparsity levels, characterized by the number of azimuth and polar angles
(64,8)
,
(128,12)
and
(256,16)
, and various noise levels of 0.01 and 0.05, are considered. The minimization of the objective function (
14
) is performed for different fixed values of
\lambda
. Only the reconstruction results with the smallest
relative reconstruction accuracy
from the data with 0.05 noise are shown in Figure
2
. Naturally, the more measurements the better results, and all the maximum values of error images are below 0.15. In Figure
3
, the
relative reconstruction accuracy
and regularization term
